**LATEST P1 SOURCE INCREMENT (28 OCT 2026) — CONSENT BEFORE DATASET UPLOAD; OWNER WINDOWS QA OPEN:** Continued from owner-accepted **24/24 Windows direct-primary-project-load test + TypeScript/Vite build**, accepted earlier **49/49 external project/route/restore** and Chrome edited-dock URL Cancel plus two-tab project retention. The remaining P1 problem was `frontend/src/App.tsx::startUpload()` calling **`upload(file)` BEFORE** any consent about losing the current edited dock, with authorization only at `loadProject(newId)` after the backend had created a new project. **New bounded source on branch `feature/live-dev-workspace-20261023`:** Primary `App.tsx` accepts `onBeforePrimaryProjectUpload?:()=>boolean` from `UnifiedShell.tsx`, which validates workspaceReady and reuses the existing `confirmEditedDockDiscard('switch-project')` BEFORE `setLoading`, API upload, refresh or project switch. Cancel makes **no upload request and leaves current project, edited dock and selected file intact**. On approved upload `loadProject(data.id,approvedBeforeUpload)` uses prior consent **only if the current primary project has not changed** (tracked in `liveProjectId` ref), so it does not demand a second approval. Shell **does not clear the edited dock marker at initial consent**; the new `onPrimaryUploadedProjectCommitted` hook clears it **only once new project reads have succeeded**, preventing failed imports from leaving the old dock unguarded. `loadProject` now **fetches the replacement project and live profile BEFORE clearing previous project state** so failed API reads preserve old project and draft. `uploadViewMounted` guards stop a completed upload from activating a project if its initiating App unmounted; the backend-created project is not retroactively deleted. Post-upload failures retain existing honest `Dataset was imported but was not opened` notice and clear the upload file/overlay, avoiding accidental duplicate import. No automatic Quality approval, data cleaning, model training, AI calls or installer changes. `frontend/tests/p1-preupload-consent.test.cjs` adds **14 authored cases** covering pre-network Cancel, one approval, upload failure, post-upload failed load, stale consent, unmount, missing file, embedded/standalone, order and no arbitrary form-value persistence. ## P1 PROJECT CATALOGUE CONCURRENT FETCH GUARD — SOURCE VERIFIED, WINDOWS ACCEPTANCE PENDING (27 OCTOBER 2026)

**GitHub authority:** `Sonkhe-Shongwe/PilotODIN`, branch `feature/live-dev-workspace-20261023`, verify current HEAD again on entry. The **synthetic Region trim at Quality working v1 is already approved** (5 Region cells, v0 original preserved); no repeated approval, training, source cleaning, export, fonts or installer is requested. The prior Windows **9/9 P1 Quality handoff tests + Vite build**, old 7/7 category label tests, scoped P0 API/browser and P2 accepted report micro-gates remain CLOSED.

**New concrete reliability issue discovered in live GitHub `frontend/src/UnifiedShell.tsx`:** `refreshProjects()` can run concurrently after mount, project-change events and storage updates. Before this patch, an older `get('/projects')` response (or failure) could arrive later and overwrite the more recent catalogue, readiness and warning state; the existing `shouldClearUnavailableProject` protected *different-ID* selections but did not sequence multiple responses with the **same selected project ID**. This is a **source-supported asynchronous race exposure**, not a claim of an owner-observed actual project loss. The objective is to avoid stale project-list replies falsifying availability after a newly created/selected project or switching.

**Bounded implementation:** `frontend/src/projectCatalogRequestGuard.ts` implements a latest-issued-only request token with `begin`, `accepts`, and `invalidate`; never stores project IDs or dataset content. `UnifiedShell.tsx` starts a token before every catalogue fetch, checks it after success and catch **before** modifying `projects`, `catalogState`, warning or clearing an unavailable project, and invalidates on shell unmount. Existing localStorage project selection, project-specific tabs/dock layout, versioned data and the `shouldClearUnavailableProject` same-selection check remain unchanged. An isolated check caught an unissued-token acceptance if `invalidate()` was called before any request in the first draft; **fixed before acceptance** using separate `nextRequest` / `activeRequest` counters, and added a regression case.

**Executed evidence for the current corrected helper:** Local helper source had a byte-identical Git blob `59e45b2a9c110a6a1daf42d4c502f6369a7cd6e5` matching live GitHub `projectCatalogRequestGuard.ts`. **7/7 isolated replay checks PASS**, covering old and new same-project requests, reversed fulfilment/error order, fresh token after unmount, phantom unissued token, and separate guard instances. Strict `tsc --noEmit --strict --target ES2021 --module ESNext` for exact helper **PASS**. Re-fetched actual `UnifiedShell.tsx` and verified guard import, `useRef`, request start, success/failure checks, unmount invalidation, and retention of `shouldClearUnavailableProject`. The new **8-case** committed `frontend/tests/p1-project-catalogue-race.test.cjs` includes full shell source integration and has **NOT** been run in a current full frontend checkout. Full Vite Windows build / real project-switching validation for this patch are **NOT YET ACCEPTED**; do not imply CI or user Windows tests passed.

**NEXT scoped Windows gate — batch, do not repeat accepted suites:** After safe pull (preserve uncommitted files), from `frontend` run `node --test tests/p1-project-catalogue-race.test.cjs tests/p1-quality-preview-whitespace.test.cjs` and then `npm run build`. These are the **two new, not previously Windows accepted** P1 changes only (8 + 6 authored test cases); all previously accepted Node/ML/P2 suites remain closed. If green, in existing Live Dev, switch between two *already existing* projects (e.g. `P0_SYNTHETIC_UI_ML_80` and another project) and verify correct active project/content and no spurious unavailable-project warning; no import or model training. For preview formatter, optionally open and **Cancel** read-only Undo proposal in the synthetic Quality v1 (never Approve Undo): Before/After should now reveal `[SPACE]` on the whitespace-bearing side. ## SAVED HTML PREVIEW FIRST WINDOWS TEST: 5/6, TEST HARNESS BUG FIXED IN SOURCE, RECHECK PENDING (27 OCTOBER 2026)

**Owner actual Windows evidence:** User executed `node --test tests/p2-saved-report-html-preview.test.cjs` from Windows PowerShell after pulling the feature branch. Console showed **6 tests, 5 PASS, 1 FAIL, 0 skipped**. The only failure was named `Preview refuses active HTML content even though frame is sandboxed`, at `frontend/tests/p2-saved-report-html-preview.test.cjs:52`, `AssertionError ERR_ASSERTION: Missing expected exception`. Do not call the feature accepted or infer the TypeScript/Vite build passed from this cropped test screenshot. Do not repeat any accepted P2 font/sidecar/unicode/28-case suites.

**Exact root cause found without changing production code:** Two distinct new test loops accidentally used JavaScript `for (const ... of (sample1, sample2, ...))`. The parentheses create a **comma expression**, evaluating to only the final sample string, then `for...of` iterates its individual characters, rather than each malicious/malformed HTML sample. The active-content test inserted one character at a time and incorrectly expected rejection. The previously green malformed-document test also did NOT exercise all its samples; its PASS was an incomplete guard test, not proof of coverage. Source `frontend/src/reportSavedPreview.ts` already rejects all seven actual active-element/refresh samples under its deny-list, and no failure in production HTML preview was demonstrated by this screenshot.

**Targeted CORRECTION COMMITTED:** `frontend/tests/p2-saved-report-html-preview.test.cjs` now has explicit `samples=[...]` arrays, loops over every item, individually asserts the expected rejection with payload-specific error messages, and checks the counters: **5 malformed/oversized + 7 active-content sample cases**. The suite still contains the same 6 top-level Node tests; these additional cases are internal assertions, not 12 new top-level tests. GitHub static inspection confirmed the incorrect `for..of (...)` forms are gone and all seven current explicit active tags match the existing preview filter. **The corrected six-test suite is SOURCE COMMITTED, not yet owner Windows executed.** Production TSX, helper, HTML/PDF API and rendering code were not changed during this fix.

**Engineering proof improved with concurrency hazard detection:** ReportLab 4.4.9 locally generated wrong ToUnicode destination `<1F4C8>` for U+1F4C8; UTF-16BE surrogate destination `<D83DDCC8>` extracted as exact emoji with identical rendered pixel hash. A newly discovered hazard: `pdfmetrics.registerFont` aliases dynamic fonts by internal face name. An ordinary font registered first can silently defeat a later subclass under a different external name. Baseline and corrected proofs therefore **must be isolated in separate subprocesses** and the font registration checked for object identity. Local subprocess proof is PASS with an available developer font, but the user's staged font and installed ReportLab have NOT yet executed this new class.

**New source-only helper, NOT used by production render path:** `backend/pdf_unicode_cmap.py` (commit `a3582a1a302755a85b398aa2d269b2c95ecbd7ad`) contains `DocumentUnicodeTTFont.addObjects` which repairs only new PDF-document font ToUnicode streams without touching module globals, and a fail-closed checked font-registration helper. Conservative CMap conversion preserves BMP and correct 8-hex mappings, rejects malformed/invalid/oversized content. The old `backend/reports.py` and `backend/report_charts.py`, calculations, evidence, SQL, report revisions, font binary bundle and native Tauri assets have **not been modified**.

## NEWEST P2 CHECKPOINT — Unicode font compatibility PROTOTYPED; bundled-font production fix not yet implemented (26 October 2026)

**Live GitHub branch** `feature/live-dev-workspace-20261023`, HEAD before this handoff commit `ebf1be77e153342f969b47b9626bdf83b66944ac`. Fetch live HEAD before any next modification. None of those gates were repeated, no reports/user data/model was altered.

**Niche research confirmed:** Official Noto Sans and Noto Sans SC distribution licenses are SIL OFL 1.1 (the SC OFL explicitly names Adobe's Reserved Font Name `Source`). Noto Sans SC variable TTF distribution is ~16.9MB and cannot be assumed compatible with our pinned ReportLab regular-weight/static API. Official ReportLab documents `TTFont` + `pdfmetrics.registerFont` for embedded Unicode. Noto Emoji mono has partial emoji coverage; a CJK/emoji fallback and transparent unsupported-character policy is required. Font license terms require including copyright/license notices with redistributed binaries and avoiding prohibited reserved names on derivatives.

**Local compatibility proof, not Windows acceptance:** In an isolated non-user environment, registering an available Noto Sans Regular TTF with ReportLab yielded a PDF with embedded TrueType subset; PyMuPDF exact-text extraction preserved `café`, `São Paulo`, `R²`, `Δ Ω`, `Москва` and `₹`. Font cmap contains these but lacks `漢`, `字` and `📈`. Therefore a single sans font will not solve the CJK/emoji defects. No source font binary was copied or committed.

**NEW developer-only utility:** `tools/p2_pdf_font_coverage_probe.py` (commit `3eca7e2c7ba32c12217c06e08e930cb599ce2e18`) accepts explicit local regular/CJK/emoji font candidate paths, checks ReportLab char-to-glyph coverage, creates a transient synthetic PDF in memory and verifies exact extracted sample text. Reports basename/bytes/SHA-256 and supported/unsupported statuses, with clear disclaimers: not visual quality, license approval, owner Windows or release acceptance. This is NOT a new owner BAT and should not be requested of the user until exact approved assets are selected. Updated authoritative research/plan `docs/P2_PDF_UNICODE_FONT_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md` (commit `ebf1be77e153342f969b47b9626bdf83b66944ac`) records official references, measured first-font PoC, SC size, missing CJK/emoji and remaining gates.

**What remains OPEN:** Exact pinned OFL font assets with SHA-256 and license copies, independent static TrueType compatibility for Noto SC, CJK/emoji policies, robust multi-font splitting across all PDF paragraph/table and vector-chart drawing text, deterministic offline sidecar asset packaging, negative unsupported-glyph handling, Windows 8GB constraints, targeted renderer tests and actual visual acceptance. **Do not claim Unicode fixed.** No font binaries have been committed; production `backend/reports.py`, `backend/report_charts.py`, existing launcher/tests and Tauri resources are unchanged in this increment.

## Project identity, environment and protected constraints

PilotODIN 1.0: **local-first, single-user Windows desktop data analytics and ML platform**; React/TypeScript/Vite frontend and Tauri/Rust native shell; Python/FastAPI, SQLite, pandas/sklearn analytics; future optional licensed **local** Qwen inference (not bundled/proven yet). Preserve immutable uploaded originals, versioned working data, explicit Quality approvals, honest deterministic statistics, leakage-safe split/target selection, project isolation and end-to-end report provenance. Explicitly **the user chooses the supervised prediction target**; target suggestion/automatic Master Analyst/Scientist remain research/planned, not shipped.

User's Windows clone uses **GitHub Desktop** on the active feature branch, `Start_PilotODIN_Live_Dev.bat` for development (frontend localhost:5173, backend localhost:8000), and `.venv-live\Scripts\python.exe`. The separate Tauri DEV ONLY smoke window was already compiled/opened and validated for native Save As. `PILOTODIN_HOME` and test `tmp_path` must isolate regression data. Do not launch installer, GitHub Actions (hosted minutes exhausted), or a high-RAM model spontaneously. Don't discard user's uncommitted files; past tests reported a dirty local checkout.

If memory observation unavailable, defer new work in Low-memory. Floors are **heuristics, not peak memory guarantees**. Adaptive/Performance retain all existing hard resource/integrity caps.

## ACCEPTED P5 SOURCE BATCH — COOPERATIVE CANCELLATION + SOFT TIMEOUT OWNER GATE PASS (25 October 2026)

**New source implementation:** [P5 cooperative training cancellation](./P5_COOPERATIVE_TRAINING_CANCELLATION.md). Previous P5 stages (single Low-memory ML slot with 25/25 backend + 3/3 Node; factual training progress with 18/18 backend + 7/7 Node + Vite) are already **owner-verified** and require no separate replay.

- `backend/storage.py` adds a backwards-compatible SQLite `experiment_run_controls` table storing **cancel_requested** and a **deadline_at** timestamp if an explicit duration was chosen. A queued cancel immediately sets status `cancelled`, releases the one-training slot, appends an actual terminal event and prevents worker claim. A running cancel **only records a request**; worker status stays `running` until its safe checkpoint actually stops it. Cancellation/completed-commit decisions serialize via SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` to avoid false success. New terminal events `cancelled` and `timed_out`; completed experiment results preserved.
- `backend/engine.py::run_training` now accepts an **optional** stop callback, checking before/after baseline CV and each candidate CV/fit. It rethrows the stop interruption rather than classifying the model as failed. sklearn individual `cross_val_score`/`fit` remains **blocking and not forcibly interrupted**.
- `backend/main.py::train_background` checks interruptions between work phases and artifact dumps, refuses a final completed commit after a stop/expired deadline, and deletes only the new incomplete `models/<eid>` directory on cancellation/soft timeout/failed write. An abrupt OS crash can still leave inert incomplete files; no atomic filesystem commit guarantee. New local-only `POST /api/experiments/{eid}/cancel`, a Pydantic-validated `max_duration_seconds` request (null or 60..14400 sec) stored per experiment, additive `control` metadata on GET.
- `frontend/src/App.tsx` now offers an **optional** Model Lab soft training limit (No limit default / 10 / 30 / 60 minutes) and distinct **Cancel queued training** vs **Request safe cancellation** control on Evaluate, with explicit pending notice until a worker checkpoint; distinct cancelled/time-limit terminal messages. Existing polling is reused and protects project/experiment identity. `frontend/src/api.ts` adds types.
- **New focused tests** `tests/test_p5_training_cancellation.py` (queued cancel, pending running cancel, deadline, no implicit timeout, interruption during fitting/saving, cleanup of partial writes, preservation of completed files, cancellation propagation through engine, invalid timeout); `frontend/tests/p5-training-cancellation-contracts.test.cjs` (5 source contracts). Updated one previous P5 progress source test to accept a multiline engine invocation. New suite added once to future comprehensive runner.
- **Owner Windows execution completed and actual ZIP extracted:** `pilotodin-p5-cancel-ayag0sci.zip`, JSON at **2026-10-25T18:38:46.030809Z**, three phases all exit **0**, overall PASS. Backend cancellation + affected P5/P1 synthetic Pytests **45/45 PASS in 5.05s**; Node source contracts **12/12 PASS**, no failure/skip; TypeScript + Vite build **PASS**, 1,618 modules, 3.47s. Non-blocking Vite chunk-size warning. No original project datasets/model artifacts were altered by tests. No need to rerun P5. The runner JSON's inherited `durable_general_scheduler_or_safe_cancellation: NOT_IMPLEMENTED` conflates distinct features: **safe cooperative cancellation passed; general durable scheduler remains unimplemented**.
- **Scope:** cooperative stop only, not guaranteed time-to-stop; no arbitrary durable task queue, resumable ML, forced estimator kill, installed application or real offline Qwen+8GB E2 certification. Existing dataset approval, model caps, leakage and project version isolation are unchanged.

**Sequencing decision after the green P5 owner gate:** Defer advanced P5/P7 global task scheduler, worker subprocess preemption and cross-Qwen orchestration. The user's authoritative complexity-last roadmap prioritizes the simpler deterministic **P3 semantic understanding, then P4 target opportunity recommendations**, before Master modes and heavy autonomy. P5 foundation is accepted for today's bounded ML workflows.

## CURRENT: P2 SAVE AS DIALOG FILENAME MISMATCH TRACED TO NON-EXPOSED CORS HEADER; SOURCE FIX PENDING WINDOWS QA (26 OCTOBER 2026)

**Newest authoritative checkpoint.** Owner provided File Explorer evidence that the new DevQA `exports` folder contains generic `pilotodin_report.html` / `pilotodin_report.pdf`. A second owner screenshot of the **actual Windows Tauri Save As dialog** proves that `pilotodin_report.html` is the **automatically proposed default**, not a filename manually chosen by the owner. Expected for this synthetic saved revision: versioned `pilotodin_report_1df9c2357c56_rev2.html` and `pilotodin_report_1df9c2357c56_rev2.pdf`. Therefore **do not mark current full native export as PASS**, or ask user to rename files; current run must be truthfully cancelled/closed and kept as failure evidence. Previously verified P2 seed/FastAPI/HTTP digests/TS/Vite/native executable startup remain narrowly PASS.

**Root-cause source investigation (high confidence, not yet proven by a new Windows run):** `backend/main.py` emits proper `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename=\"pilotodin_report_<id>_rev<revision>.<format>\"` for each real report export, but CORSMiddleware previously did **not** set `expose_headers`. Because Vite's React WebView2 origin `http://127.0.0.1:5176` is cross-origin to the isolated Uvicorn port, frontend `response.headers.get('content-disposition')` returned null and `frontend/src/api.ts` silently used `pilotodin_report.<format>`. The production Rust Tauri `save_report_as` calls `.set_file_name(filename)` and therefore displayed the fallback in the real Windows dialog. The original TestClient verified server header existence but **not browser exposure**. This is exactly the kind of cross-layer error that predictive QA needs to catch.

**Minimal cross-layer correction SOURCE COMMITTED, owner Windows QA still PENDING:**
- `backend/main.py` exposes only `Content-Disposition` through existing allowed origin CORS; existing origins and authentication constraints unchanged.
- `frontend/src/api.ts` requires an explicit report ID and frozen revision in the export path, checks the exact server-delivered versioned filename, and **fails before opening native Save As** when the header is hidden/missing or wrong. No silent generic fallback; MIME/signature checks retained, Rust unchanged.
- `tools/Seed_P2_Native_Live_Chain_Local.py` simulates **actual Vite origin** in its real FastAPI TestClient calls; checks `access-control-allow-origin` and browser-visible `access-control-expose-headers: content-disposition` on BOTH HTML and PDF before allowing native launch. Existing repeatable ReportLab metadata, real live HTTP byte digest and final on-disk SHA requirements not lowered.
- A new P2 source-contract case checks CORS, seed browser origin/exposure, frontend fail-closed behavior and native Rust filename flow; P2 focused contract tests increase from **5 to 6**, and foundation risk register now permanently adds **HTTP-01** (browser-visible metadata at protocol boundary, not only raw API header), with related synthetic test expectation adjusted.
- `docs/P2_DEVQA_NATIVE_CHAIN_ACCEPTANCE.md` and `docs/DEVQA_PREDICTIVE_FRAMEWORK.md` record the discovery and risk control. Current P2 runner BAT unchanged, historical test evidence preserved.

**ONE exact next owner action AFTER source pulled and current old run closed:** Pull current feature branch safely, preserving five local untracked Rust/Tauri generated files (do not discard/commit automatically). Run `Run_PilotODIN_P2_DevQA_Native_Chain.bat` ONCE. Its seeded cross-origin header test must PASS **before** opening any native Save As, then the Windows HTML/PDF dialogs must actually suggest the exact versioned filenames. Save both into newly printed DevQA `exports` directory, don't rename or overwrite old generic files, verify exact SHA. Green share console, red upload `P2-NATIVE-CHAIN-*.zip` and relevant screenshot. **New source has NOT been executed on owner Windows; no claim of fixed application acceptance yet.** Other P5 general scheduler, P6/P7, E2 licensed offline Qwen under combined measured 8 GB, P8 E-01–E-24 still open.

## CURRENT P2 LIVE NATIVE-CHAIN GATE STOPPED AT PDF RENDER NONDETERMINISM; ISOLATED FIX COMMITTED, OWNER RERUN PENDING (26 OCTOBER 2026)

**Most recent authoritative checkpoint; supersedes older next-step instructions.** Owner uploaded `pilotodin-p2-native-chain-56e9e41218ce.zip` and console screenshot following the reserved-`$HOME` repair. The actual ZIP was independently opened: `result.json` says **`p2-synthetic-real-report-seed: PASS`, `p2-isolated-real-fastapi: PASS`, `overall: FAIL`**, message `Live HTTP pdf content differs from frozen synthetic fixture.` Uvicorn log confirms real GET revision 2, HTML revision 2 **200**, PDF revision 2 **200**. Failure occurred at **exact PDF SHA match between seeder's ReportLab-rendered PDF and the subsequently rendered live-Uvicorn PDF**, before TS/Vite build, Tauri native dev window or real HTML/PDF Save As tests. No claim of corrupted PDF bytes or production export defect is supported by this evidence.

**Root-cause mechanism independently reproduced:** ReportLab default PDF generation includes the render-time CreationDate/ModDate and document identifier. Separate renders of identical report data can produce different byte SHA-256, despite frozen report revision/content. A minimal ReportLab probe locally reproduced different SHA-256 for the same PDF when rendered at different times, and demonstrated **identical SHA-256 under documented `RL_invariant=1` configuration**. The owner ZIP does not include PDF response bytes for independent confirmation that metadata is the *only* differing field. Therefore label it **most likely nondeterministic PDF rendering at a strict test-only byte-equivalence checkpoint**, not definitively proven corrupt or a different report.

**Minimal test-only repair committed (P2 full-chain ONLY):** In `tools/Verify_P2_Native_Live_Report_Local.ps1`, save prior `RL_invariant` environment value and set `$env:RL_invariant='1'` **before spawning BOTH seeder and isolated Uvicorn**, restore it in existing cleanup. Production `backend/reports.py`, Tauri Rust `save_report_as`, React `ReportWorkspace`, saved SQLite revisions and other routes untouched. ## Operating protocol for every further ChatGPT session

1. **Verify repository HEAD and read this file plus current QA comments** before changing source or reporting status. New-chat summaries are pointers, not substitutes for code and test evidence.
2. Make coherent small development **batches** with tests; write to active feature branch, not main. Keep original datasets/data history intact; **never launch GitHub Actions or final installer** without explicit authorization.
3. Use user-visible Windows verification only when truly necessary because GitHub connector can commit/review but **cannot execute Rust/Tauri on user's laptop**. Prefer **one** focused launcher and one ZIP per development package; never claim an unrun test passed.
4. Record after each meaningful batch: **latest source commit**, code vs tests vs actual owner evidence, open blockers, **exact next action** in this file and QA Issue #2. Reconcile older docs; historical logs are not current to-do lists.
5. **Privacy/access:** This is a private GitHub repository. A new chat needs GitHub connector permissions to read it. Chat-only screenshots and local Temp ZIP paths do not automatically transfer to a different chat; status is recorded here, and artifacts must be supplied again only if the new task requires inspecting their contents. Original DOCX blueprint may need Project file access for new architecture work.
6. Avoid follow-up questions if facts already in this handoff answer them. Ask only if a truly essential authorization, missing resource or ambiguous requirement prevents safe work. **Zero questions cannot be guaranteed** across missing permissions, local UI observation or external changes.
